With `--candidate`, only publications and jobs are filtered; activations and canonical computation history remain global.\n61\t- `metrics` returns `{\"evaluator_invocations\": integer, \"publication_count\": integer, \"job_count\": integer, \"seed_result_count\": integer, \"result_count\": integer}`.\n62\t\n63\tPublication and activation identifiers are opaque, unique, and monotonically ordered by their creation events. Queue processing order is FIFO. `process-one` is deterministic and never waits for future work.\n64\t\n65\t## HTTP\n66\t\n67\t`python -m evalstack.api` serves:\n68\t\n69\t- `GET /health` -> `200 {\"status\":\"ok\"}`.\n70\t- `POST /v1/evaluations` with `{\"candidate_id\": string}` -> `202` and exactly the `submit` response schema; missing/invalid state -> `409 {\"error\": string}`.\n71\t- `GET /v1/leaderboard` -> `200` with:\n72\t\n73\t```text\n74\t{\n75\t  \"contract_content_ref\": string|null,\n76\t  \"entries\": [\n77\t    {\n78\t      \"candidate_id\": string,\n79\t      \"publication_id\": string,\n80\t      \"checkpoint_content_ref\": string,\n81\t      \"contract_content_ref\": string|null,\n82\t      \"status\": \"pending\"|\"complete\",\n83\t      \"score\": integer|null\n84\t    },\n85\t    ... sorted by candidate_id\n86\t  ]\n87\t}\n88\t```\n89\t\n90\tWhen there is no active contract, `contract_content_ref` is null and every published candidate is pending. Commands emit exactly one JSON object on stdout and diagnostics on stderr. A nonzero exit means the operation failed.\n\n## Content formats and score\n\nA checkpoint JSON object has this schema:\n\n```text\n{\n  \"format\": \"tabular-policy-v1\",\n  \"run_id\": string,\n  \"checkpoint_name\": string,\n  \"actions\": { observation: integer, ... }\n}\n```\n\nAn evaluation-contract JSON object has this schema:\n\n```text\n{\n  \"format\": \"evaluation-contract-v1\",\n  \"suite_name\": string,\n  \"dataset_revision\": string,\n  \"evaluator_revision\": string,\n  \"aggregation\": \"sum\",\n  \"seeds\": [\n    {\n      \"seed\": integer,\n      \"cases\": [\n        {\"observation\": string, \"expected_action\": integer, \"reward\": integer},\n        ...\n      ]\n    },\n    ...\n  ]\n}\n```\n\nSeed score is the sum of `reward` for cases where the checkpoint action for `observation` equals `expected_action`; missing observations do not match. Canonical score is the sum of all seed scores. Seed integers are unique within a contract. Every field and nested value in either JSON document is part of its content identity, including labels such as `run_id`, `checkpoint_name`, and `suite_name`. Identity reference strings are opaque to callers.\n\n## CLI\n\nRun commands as `evalctl <operation>` or `python -m evalstack.cli <operation>`.\n\n- `init [--reset]` creates the schema and returns `{\"ok\": boolean}`. `--reset` is an administrative test helper that clears application state.\n- `publish --candidate ID --checkpoint ABSOLUTE_JSON_PATH` appends a publication and returns `{\"candidate_id\": string, \"publication_id\": string, \"checkpoint_content_ref\": string}`.\n- `activate-contract --contract ABSOLUTE_JSON_PATH` appends a contract activation and returns `{\"activation_id\": string, \"contract_content_ref\": string}`.\n- `submit --candidate ID` accepts the candidate's current publication under the active contract. It returns `{\"job_id\": string, \"candidate_id\": string, \"publication_id\": string, \"checkpoint_content_ref\": string, \"contract_content_ref\": string, \"status\": \"queued\"|\"complete\", \"reused\": boolean}`. Missing current state is an error.\n- `process-one` handles at most one queued seed delivery and returns `{\"status\": \"empty\"}` or `{\"status\": \"processed\"|\"duplicate\"|\"complete\", \"job_id\": string, \"seed\": integer|null, \"checkpoint_content_ref\": string, \"contract_content_ref\": string, \"score\": integer|null}`. It may enqueue remaining seed work.\n- `retry-one --job ID --seed INTEGER` enqueues a delivery for an existing accepted job and returns `{\"job_id\": string, \"seed\": integer, \"status\": \"queued\"}`.\n- `clear-result-cache` deletes only disposable completed-result cache entries and returns `{\"deleted\": integer}`. It must not delete pending queue deliveries.\n- `leaderboard` returns the same object as `GET /v1/leaderboard`.\n- `inspect [--candidate ID]` returns `{\"publications\": array, \"activations\": array, \"jobs\": array, \"seed_results\": array, \"results\": array}`. The row schemas are:\n  - publication: `{\"publication_id\": string, \"publication_seq\": integer, \"candidate_id\": string, \"checkpoint_content_ref\": string, \"checkpoint_label\": string}`;\n  - activation: `{\"activation_id\": string, \"activation_seq\": integer, \"contract_content_ref\": string, \"contract_label\": string}`;\n  - job: `{\"job_id\": string, \"job_seq\": integer, \"candidate_id\": string, \"publication_id\": string, \"checkpoint_content_ref\": string, \"contract_content_ref\": string, \"checkpoint_label\": string, \"contract_label\": string, \"status\": \"queued\"|\"running\"|\"complete\", \"reused\": boolean, \"resolved_checkpoint_content_ref\": string|null, \"resolved_contract_content_ref\": string|null, \"completed_seq\": integer|null}`;\n  - seed result: `{\"checkpoint_content_ref\": string, \"contract_content_ref\": string, \"seed\": integer, \"score\": integer, \"source_job_id\": string}`;\n  - canonical result: `{\"checkpoint_content_ref\": string, \"contract_content_ref\": string, \"score\": integer, \"seed_count\": integer, \"completed_by_job_id\": string, \"completed_seq\": integer}`.\n  Extra diagnostic fields are allowed on rows. With `--candidate`, only publications and jobs are filtered; activations and canonical computation history remain global.\n- `metrics` returns `{\"evaluator_invocations\": integer, \"publication_count\": integer, \"job_count\": integer, \"seed_result_count\": integer, \"result_count\": integer}`.\n\nPublication and activation identifiers are opaque, unique, and monotonically ordered by their creation events. Queue processing order is FIFO. `process-one` is deterministic and never waits for future work.\n\n## HTTP\n\n`python -m evalstack.api` serves:\n\n- `GET /health` -> `200 {\"status\":\"ok\"}`.\n- `POST /v1/evaluations` with `{\"candidate_id\": string}` -> `202` and exactly the `submit` response schema; missing/invalid state -> `409 {\"error\": string}`.\n- `GET /v1/leaderboard` -> `200` with:\n\n```text\n{\n  \"contract_content_ref\": string|null,\n  \"entries\": [\n    {\n      \"candidate_id\": string,\n      \"publication_id\": string,\n      \"checkpoint_content_ref\": string,\n      \"contract_content_ref\": string|null,\n      \"status\": \"pending\"|\"complete\",\n      \"score\": integer|null\n    },\n    ... sorted by candidate_id\n  ]\n}\n```\n\nWhen there is no active contract, `contract_content_ref` is null and every published candidate is pending. This is the core bug—the label-based approach doesn't account for content variations, allowing different versions to overwrite each other in the cache.\n\nThe fix is straightforward: build the cache key from the actual content references instead of the labels. But there's a second bug layered on top: process_one is resolving checkpoint and contract references at delivery time by looking up the current publication and active contract, rather than using the snapshotted content references already stored on the job itself. The comment in worker.py reveals this was intentional—keeping queue messages small by deferring resolution—but it's the wrong approach. We need to use the job's snapshot fields directly, which means the publication and contract variables become unnecessary since we're no longer deriving labels from them.\n\nNow checking invariant 4 about append-only checkpoint history: the leaderboard query fetches the active contract and then retrieves each candidate's latest publication, which correctly represents the current publication under the active contract. This part looks sound.\n\nBut there's a critical issue in how the query selects the job result—it's grabbing the most recently completed job for a candidate regardless of whether that job's checkpoint and contract references match the candidate's current publication and the active contract. This violates invariant 4, which requires the leaderboard to show only completed work for the specific combination of the current publication's checkpoint content and the active contract's content, not just any recent job completion.\n\nThe fix is simpler: query the canonical_results table directly using the current publication's checkpoint_content_ref and the active contract's contract_content_ref as keys. If a result exists for that exact pair, mark it complete with its score; otherwise, it's pending. This approach eliminates the ordering dependency entirely and ensures the leaderboard always reflects the right publication-contract combination.\n\nImplementing the leaderboard function... Verifying that the canonical results keyed by content reference handle republishing correctly—if checkpoint content matches an old result, it gets reused directly without redundant work. The publications table remains append-only, so history is preserved. Checking that the current_publication definition elsewhere in the codebase uses MAX(publication_seq) consistently, and confirming the inspect() job schema already has the resolved reference fields needed.\n\nNow I'm verifying process_one's duplicate detection logic—the seed_results primary key on (checkpoint_content_ref, contract_content_ref, seed) prevents duplicate contributions, and duplicate deliveries return a \"duplicate\" status without re-inserting, which works correctly once the job snapshot references are fixed. Looking at retry-one to see how it enqueues deliveries for existing accepted jobs. Since the job already has snapshotted checkpoint and contract references, I don't need to query current_publication or active_contract in process_one at all—just fetch the documents directly using those refs. I'm switching from a label-based cache key to a content-ref-based one that combines checkpoint and contract references, which ties the cache directly to content identity. Now I need to update the scheduler's cache lookup to use content refs instead of labels.\n\nBut there's a deeper issue: if Redis cache is cleared, submit() will create a duplicate job even though Postgres already has the canonical result. That violates the durability invariant—I should check Postgres directly for completed results, not just Redis, so duplicate submissions return \"complete\" immediately without forcing reevaluation. optimization layer for repeated lookups of the same result, avoiding redundant database queries. So the fix is straightforward: have submit() query canonical_results directly from Postgres to get the authoritative status, which naturally satisfies the invariant and the CLI contract without needing Redis involvement at all. So Redis cache is purely an optimization for submit() to avoid creating unnecessary queued jobs when results are already cached. If the cache is lost, submit() will conservatively queue a job anyway, but process_one will immediately resolve it from postgres without re-evaluating, so no work is duplicated or lost—this satisfies the invariant and seems acceptable.\n\nNow I'm wondering whether submit() should also check postgres canonical_results directly as a fallback, not just Redis, for better consistency and efficiency. The key constraint is avoiding forced reevaluation, which is already satisfied since process_one's fast path skips the evaluator call. I think fixing the cache key to use content refs instead of labels should be sufficient, but I need to verify whether checking only Redis is acceptable or if I should add a postgres lookup too. So the real bugs are: process_one resolving checkpoint/contract from current database state instead of the job's snapshot (violates invariant 1), the leaderboard using \"latest completed job\" instead of querying canonical results for the current publication/contract pair (violates invariant 4), and the cache key being label-based instead of content-ref-based, which causes false-positive reuse across different content with identical labels (violates invariant 2). The submit() function leaving a resubmission as \"queued\" when Redis misses is actually acceptable behavior per the spec — the real corruption happens downstream when process_one eventually processes that delivery using wrong state, and when the leaderboard incorrectly displays stale completed work. Bug B's label-key collision is particularly insidious because it means all four combinations of checkpoint/contract pairs with matching suite labels would collide in the cache, creating widespread
